Priced with `price_items.py --all-tlds` against merged260827 sampling DISTINCT DOMAINS: 22 captures give 268 in-window pairs over **266 distinct domains, 212 already held AT the artifact's own year (79.1%)**, pre-split 56 pairs / 35.0 EE, **post-split 36 pairs / 22.2 EE**, all at 2001, com 33 / net 3, mean weight 0.6172, typo bound 28.6%. That is **0.083 net-new post-split EE per listed domain, 5x worse than the 0.386 a held `.com` is worth at 2001 population-wide** and close to the 0.041 measured on a 2001 magazine archive, because an advertiser roster is the commercial head and we already cover the head at 2001. The 8 kanoodle and 1 bay9 pages not fetched are another ~150 domains, under 15 EE. Five method results: (1) **never guess a query-string URL; take the exact hrefs out of a captured page of the same host and probe those** - guessed URLs manufacture false zeros, and that one switch turned a flat zero into 19/20 on ah-ha; (2) `archive.org/wayback/available` is a per-URL existence oracle that never touches the metered CDX and is exact on the whole query string including odd path params like `;$sessionid$X`; (3) **but it emits REPRODUCIBLE false zeros, so it cannot close a family on its own** - `partner.ah-ha.com/1000.asp?query=tickets` was reported captured at 20010603133655 in one batch, then returned no snapshot on three consecutive retries while a known-positive control answered correctly in the same batch, and replay then served that exact capture in full at 32,986 B, so **confirm every zero that matters against the replay channel** and read a batch of zeros as an upper bound on the negative (this extends the documented `services/search/v1/scrape` lie to `wayback/available`); (4) **a PPC engine's reachable corpus equals the keyword set LINKED from its crawled homepage and nothing more** - 19 of 19 linked keywords are captured, 0 of 63 unlinked common commercial keywords are, and pagination (`&P=2`) is not captured either, so size this family by counting homepage links and never by the engine's advertiser count, GoTo having had 45,000 advertisers in Q2 2001 and zero reachable pages; (5) **new pricing law: a roster of PAYING commercial sites already carries its own year at ~79%**, the squidGuard 2000-10-18 death rather than the 2001-12-18 win, so screen any advertiser roster at ~0.08 EE per listed name before downloading it. Closed: the named artifact has no in-window capture and the siblings that do are capped by what was crawled, so neither more keywords nor more engines can move it. The 22 priced captures are already on disk and worth 22.2 EE for zero further collection. |
| **2001 nonprofit portals, a FIND that closes its own family, and the head-selection law operating INSIDE one directory (2026-08-28)** | **FIND at 202.2 net-new post-split EE over 289 pairs, all at 2001, mean weight 0.6996 (org 257 / com 29 / net 3), from 19 dated pages and 601,956 B; and the family is CLOSED at that figure, because its entire enumerable population is 1,843 names against the 9,116 that 1,000 EE needs.** Class `dated_directory`, master-eligible, approval request filed with `Decision: pending`; NOT ingested. Dating rests on the Wayback capture instant alone, since no page in the family carries a usable self-date (the Foundation Center list pages print none), so the corroboration split is taken. The paying seven, all `web/<ts>id_/`: `fdncenter.org/funders/grantmaker/gws_pubch/pubch_list.html` at 20011102033013 (740 domains), `gws_priv/priv_list.html` at 20011024182703 (548), `gws_priv/priv2.html` at 20011004165617 (546, a Netscape-4 duplicate that checks priv_list is complete), `gws_corp/corp_list.html` at 20010806091046 (376), `interaction.org/members/` at 20011101195341 (149), `foundations.org/grantmakers.html` at 20011015131123 (99), `igc.org/igc/gateway/index.html` at 20011011002103 (36). Priced with `scripts/price_items.py --all-tlds` against merged260827: 1,843 distinct pairs over 1,843 domains, 1,523 already held AT 2001 (82.6%), only 29 names never held anywhere (98.43% held), pre-split 320 pairs and 223.6 EE (overstates 1.11x, do not quote), **post-split 289 pairs and 202.2 EE**, typo upper bound 37.8%. An independent duckdb screen sampling DISTINCT DOMAINS over the three fdncenter `_list` pages agrees to 0.36% (1,648 listed, 1,619 held, 1,349 carrying 2001, 268 held-and-missing at 187.43 EE against price_items' 269 at 188.1). **279 of the 289 gain domains (96.5%) are held at 2000**, so this is adjacent-year headroom and not a death gap. Rates: **0.1097 EE per listed domain, 10.6 EE per dated page fetched.** Head-selection penalty measured at 4.34x: only 15.93% of held names here lack 2001 (289/1,814) against the `.org` population's 69.15%; `.org` at weight 0.7101 makes one already-held name dated 2001 worth 0.491 EE, above `.com`'s 0.386. **The method, and it is the result: within one nonprofit directory the discriminator is whether the listed entity's website is a CORPORATE domain or a standalone nonprofit domain, and it is readable off the section name before any fetch.** The Foundation Center's four sections are one artifact family, one crawl, one page shape, one capture window, and they spread 10.1x: priv_list 548 listed / 532 held / 375 carrying 2001 / 157 gain / 109.98 EE = **0.2007 EE per listed name**; pubch_list 740 / 728 / 625 / 103 / 72.08 = 0.0974; interaction 149 / 149 / 134 / 15 / 10.65 = 0.0715; foundations.org 99 / 99 / 94 / 5 / 3.47 = 0.0351; **corp_list 376 / 373 / 362 / 11 / 7.50 = 0.0199**. The reason is visible in the names: corporate giving programs live at `abbott.com`, `aetna.com`, `adobe.com`, `agilent.com`, so 97.05% of the held ones already carry 2001, while private foundations live on their own small `.org` domains and only 70.5% do. So split a directory by listed-entity type and price only the nonprofit-domain sections; **per-arm attribution before quoting any per-item rate.** Second method find: **on any alphabet-indexed directory look for the single-page variant FIRST.** `priv1.html` offers A-to-Z browse plus one link labelled only "related document", and that link is `priv_list.html`, the complete unannotated roster of all 26 letters in one file, both cheaper (1 request against 26) and the higher-coverage surface. **Why the family dies, and it is the pre-download screen: volume and browsability are anticorrelated across this whole sector.** Every 2001 nonprofit portal with five-figure record counts put them behind a search form and has no captured multi-record surface, GuideStar ~640,000 orgs and Idealist ~20,000 being one record per request, while CharityChoice states "more than 7,000 entries in 27 categories" in its own meta description and archives only the FORM (`searchtdframe.htm` at 20011020104216, whose only captured frame is `searchtdpage.htm`). The rosters that ARE browsable are the small accreditation-shaped bodies. Measured zeros, each fetched and parsed rather than assumed: `netministries.org/see/churches/` at 20000920005932 is 58 B of "*Sorry* cannot find that church"; `idealist.org/orgs/` at 19991002025040 yields 0 external hosts; `give.org/reports/index.asp` at 20011002001937 yields 2, both `bbb.org` and `adobe.com`, because a BBB charity report links to its own internal report page and never to the charity; `oneworld.org/partners/`, `charitynet.org/` and `wango.org/` yield 0. Genuine 404s against a passing control: the whole `gws_comm` community-foundations section and `helping.org/nonprofit/`. archive.org's item store holds no bulk artifact for the family: `advancedsearch.php` on guidestar / charity directory / nonprofit directory / grantmaker web sites / foundation center, each AND `date:[1996-01-01 TO 2001-12-31]`, returns numFound 3, 11, 27, 0 and 2,500, and every in-window hit is a printed fundraising handbook or an ERIC paper. Two traps. **`archive.org/wayback/available` returned NONE for `priv_a.html`, which the replay endpoint's own 302 then resolved to 20011024145446**, reproducing the false zero recorded on 2026-08-27; the replay 302 got the same four probes right including two genuine 404s, so re-test every `available` zero against `/web/<ts>/URL` with a known-good control before recording it. And **a sub-1KB 2001 page is often a `navigator.appVersion` browser fork, not the content**: `gws_priv/priv.html` is 849 B of JavaScript branching to `priv1.html` or `priv2.html`, and a reader that took it as the section would have filed the largest arm in the family as empty. Operationally, **`web.archive.org` and `archive.org` fail independently, so use the healthy one for the question it can answer**: replay refused roughly half of all TCP connections through this run (4 of 6, 3 of 6, 4 of 6 in three timed probes, a flat ~3.4 s connect failure, no 429, no 503, no `Retry-After`) while `archive.org/wayback/available` answered 200 continuously, so existence and URL-variant mapping were done entirely on archive.org at ~1 q/s and the expensive `id_` fetches restricted to confirmed timestamps. That refusal is capacity and wants a flat 8 s retry, not exponential backoff; treating it as absence would have filed four live pages as unreachable. Do not re-test the Foundation Center Grantmaker Web Sites (`gws_comm` was never captured, and its A-Z letter pages are the same names annotated, so 78 further fetches add nothing), helping.org, GuideStar, Idealist, CharityChoice, netministries, give.org, oneworld, wango, charitynet or foundations.org. **The one thread that belongs to a different family:** `igc.org/igc/gateway/index.html` names 36 hosts of which several are `*.igc.apc.org` subdomains, the signature of a **nonprofit ISP hosting its members**, and a member list from IGC, EcoNet, PeaceNet or an ISP association is a different population from a directory of organisations that already had their own domain. |
| **Registry MEMBER rosters, and the third question the seal-roster discriminator was missing (2026-08-28)** | **CLOSED at 17.7 net-new post-split EE over 44 pairs, 0.0294 EE per listed domain, against a family whose total enumerable domain-bearing population is about 600 names.** 11 priced pages, 471,998 B, all fetched as `http://web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/...`: Nominet's `taglist.html` at 20001206101400 (194,247 B, 3,355 tag holders), five ICANN `registrars/accredited-list.html` editions spanning 20000303 to 20011201, InterNIC `alpha.html` at 20010803025008, `corenic.org/comnetorg.htm` at 20011020044926, `centr.org/members.html` at 20011211193538, and DENIC `mitglieder.shtml` plus `mitglieder2.shtml` at 20010807. Dating is `dated_directory` and rests on the capture instant alone: no member page carries a usable self-date. Priced with `scripts/price_items.py --all-tlds` against merged260827, 742 pairs over 602 domains, 674 already held, pre-split 68 pairs and 28.1 EE (overstates 1.6x, do not quote), **post-split 44 pairs and 17.7 EE**, mean weight 0.4024, com 19 / de 14 / net 5 / cn 2 / am 2 / io 1, typo upper bound 74.6%. An independent duckdb screen sampling DISTINCT DOMAINS agrees to 0.06%: at 2001, 430 listed distinct, 416 held (96.744%), 375 already carrying 2001 (87.209%), held-and-missing-2001 = 41 and **all 41 adjacent** (held 2000), 15.99 EE; at 2000, 312 listed, 302 held, 299 already at 2000, 3 pairs and 1.72 EE. The head-selection penalty reproduces exactly: 12.8% of held names lack 2001 against the population's 61.1% for `.com`, a 4.8x penalty, and the five ICANN editions show the multi-edition lever fails too. **The method, and it is the result: the seal-roster discriminator needs a third question, and it is what the roster is FOR.** A roster prints the member's own domain only when the reader has to GO to the member. ICANN, InterNIC, CORE and CENTR exist to route a registrant to a registrar, so every row is a link (109 to 159, 83, 48 and 42 absolute member hrefs). Nominet's tag list, DENIC's Mitgliederuebersicht and SIDN's deelnemers exist to record who holds a vote or a co-op share, and those rows are a legal name and a postcode. **Routing rosters carry domains, membership rosters carry legal names.** Nominet, the only member of the family with volume at 3,355 tag holders, pays 3 pairs and 1.7 EE, **0.0005 EE per member**, because it prints TAG plus REGISTERED COMPANY NAME: 141 domain-shaped tokens in 194 kB (4.2% of rows), several of them CSS class names (`a.menu`, `b.head`, `c.dp`, `g.triad`) and the rest company names that happen to contain a domain (`101cd.com Ltd`, `4u-uk.com Limited`). DENIC's second page is the one genuine domain-bearing registry roster, 149 member `www.<member>.de` URLs in one request, and prices at 177 domains, 157 held, 14 pairs and 2.2 EE at mean weight 0.1553, below the 0.4 floor. **The corollary bounds the family before any download: every roster that does print domains is an accreditation list, and accreditation is by construction small.** gTLD accreditation was 109 to 159 companies across the whole window, against the ~2,600 held `com` names that 1,000 EE requires. Two operational finds. **`archive.org/wayback/available` at a pinned `timestamp=` separates wrong-path from never-captured without touching the metered CDX**: 22 calls in 1.2 s, with `www.isi.edu/in-notes/us-domain-delegated.txt` as a positive control returning 20010606153725 in the same batch, so the four `none` rows are the hosts and not the paths. And a dating trap: **DENIC's `.shtml` pages print a server-side timestamp that is STALE.** `mitglieder.shtml` captured 20010807122242 prints `09.03.2000, 15:55:19 Uhr` and `mitglieder2.shtml` captured 20010807123326 prints `08.02.2000, 11:49:21 Uhr`, a 17-month lag, while the home page captured 20010712011748 prints `09.04.2001`. Dating that roster by its own printed date would have moved it to 2000, where it pays 0, and would have recorded a false observation; only the capture is defensible. Dead or walled, recorded so nobody probes them again: `auda.org.au` has NO in-window capture and `/registrars/` first appears 20020802093824; `cira.ca/cira/rr_choose_en`, the registrar chooser its 2001 register page links to, returns no capture at any timestamp; `nominet.org.uk/members/membership.html` at 20010815060741 says "over 2000 members" and puts them behind a password-protected member area; `sidn.nl/deelnemers.html` at 20010309022335 puts its deelnemers behind an SSL login and names none. Do not re-test Nominet's tag list or member area, DENIC's Mitgliederuebersicht, SIDN's deelnemers, CIRA's registrar chooser, auDA, CENTR, CORE, or the ICANN and InterNIC accredited-registrar directories. **The one thread this leaves open belongs to a different family:** `denic.de/doc/DENIC/mitglieder2.shtml` is the working shape of a NATIONAL ISP ASSOCIATION roster (name, postal address, phone, e-mail and website in one page), which is the same artifact an ISPA or an ISOC chapter publishes with a larger membership and a higher-weight TLD, and no such roster has been tested here. |
| **Affiliate-network merchant catalogues, closed on the field the row actually carries (2026-08-28)** | **CLOSED, and closed on the reading rather than on a completed price: the researcher was stopped with `measurement: IN PROGRESS`, so the finding file's header figure of 101.0 EE has NOTHING behind it and must not be quoted.** What was established is the part that decides the family, and it is a clean negative. The dating argument is sound and is `dated_directory` shape: the merchant row is a PROGRAM record served from the aggregator's live program database and replayed at a Wayback timestamp inside 2001, with all 59 catalogue pages resolving to a single capture at 20010602, so the artifact does assert a state at an instant the archive fixes. **It dies one step later, on what the row contains: the listing names the affiliate PROGRAM's brand, not the merchant's site.** That is the same death as a provider-run member index and the same death the hypothesis itself predicted for the redirector case, arrived at by a different route: here the outbound link is not swallowed by `bfast.com` or `linksynergy.com`, it is simply absent, because a program directory is written for an affiliate who will be handed a tracking URL and never needs the merchant's bare hostname. **The screen that generalises: ask what the directory's READER is being sent to.** A routing roster prints a domain because the reader must go to the member; an affiliate catalogue routes through the network by design, so the domain is structurally missing from the record even when the record is perfectly dated. This is the same law the registry member rosters measured on the same day, reached from the opposite side. Do not re-test Commission Junction, LinkShare, BeFree, ClickTrade or `refer-it.com` merchant catalogues. If anything here is ever worth re-opening it is the pricing that never ran, and it should be re-opened only against an artifact class that prints bare hostnames as TEXT beside the redirector anchor, which is the Bomis shape and is not what these 59 pages do. |
| **Channel-partner and reseller locator databases, closed on artifact existence in one screen (2026-08-28)** | **CLOSED at 0 EE, on the cheapest screen the hypothesis named and before any bulk fetch: nothing reaches an item, because the roster is a QUERY RESULT and no parameterised result page is in the archive.** The hypothesis was the seal-roster shape at a much larger scale, Microsoft Solution Provider and Certified Partner, Cisco, Novell, Sun, Intel and Compaq partner locators, tens of thousands of small IT firms weighted to `uk`, `au`, `ca` and `com`, and it wrote its own death test: whether the 2001 capture exposes an enumerable namespace the way BBBOnLine exposed 36 letter pages, or only a form POSTing to a CGI. It is the form. **This is the CDNnet death and it is now the third family to die of it** (CDNnet, PICS label lookup, and now partner locators), which makes it the reliable pre-download screen rather than a coincidence: a crawler cannot guess a postcode, a product code or a country code, so a search-only surface leaves no archived artifact to date, no matter how large or how well dated the database behind it was. The record here is interim in its numbers and complete in its verdict: there is no EE to measure because there is no item. **Test enumerability before value on any locator, directory or find-a-partner surface**, since it costs one capture and it is decided by the URL shape alone. Do not re-test Microsoft, Cisco, Novell, Sun, Intel or Compaq partner and reseller locators. |
| **RSS and RDF channel registries, and the head-selection penalty that INVERTS the aim-at-2001 rule (2026-08-28)** | **Closed at 47.9 net-new post-split EE over 83 pairs, all at 2001, mean weight 0.5775 (com 54, org 10, net 9, de 3, is 2, pl 1), against a whole-family ceiling under 250 EE.** Dating was never the problem: the registry dump stamps itself (`dateCreated 2001-11-29`, `updated="Fri, 09 Nov 2001 22:34:00 GMT"`) and the Wayback capture pins it, a `dated_directory`, and Syndic8's `feedlist.php` prints a per-feed Created and Approved date. The family dies on population. Syndic8, the only member that works: `web/20011112122642id_/http://www.syndic8.com/genfeed.php?Format=opml`, 861,293 B in ONE request, parsed 3,479 of 3,479 outlines (`stats.php` at 2001-11-25 corroborates 5,718 total feeds). Sampled as DISTINCT DOMAINS: 1,610 distinct registrable, 1,527 held in some year (94.8%), 1,453 held AT 2001 (90.2%), held-and-missing-2001 74, held-2000-missing-2001 71, never held 83; post-split 74 pairs and 42.3 EE at **0.0263 EE per listed domain** (pre-split 157 pairs and 88.9 EE, overstates 2.1x, do not quote). weblogs.com's `changes.xml` ping registry rates 2.5x better per domain, 106 registrable from one capture, 87 held at 2001, 11 post-split pairs and 6.9 EE at 0.0651 EE per domain, but it is killed on volume: `__wb/sparkline` reports **2 in-window captures** of `changes.xml` and 1 of `newhome.weblogs.com/`, so the union ceiling is ~250 domains and ~16 EE and the 22,490-member roster the file self-reports was never archived as a listing. Union of the two, priced against merged260827: 1,681 domains, 1,507 already held at 2001, **83 post-split pairs and 47.9 EE**. xmlTree is 1999-dated and unextractable: the page self-dates "Tuesday, 12 October 1999" and counts 1,318 leaves over 10 Dewey branches, but branch pages carry 0 external hrefs and 0 leaf links and the ColdFusion `resource/detail.cfm/ResourceID/N` pages returned the not-archived calendar for 6 of 6 sampled ids against a passing control, so 0 extractable domains. newsisfree is the right shape at the wrong cost: 41 in-window captures of `/sources/byname/`, self-dated "Thu, 11/08/01", but the letter pages print 31 ids and titles per page and **no hostnames**; `/sources/info/<id>/` does print the site URL, so it is 1 datable domain per request over ~2,700 ids, a ~175 EE ceiling for ~2,700 fetches, and the headline URLs on those pages are `link_target` and never date a year. `my.netscape.com/publish/` is a publisher onboarding form with 1 external href: the hypothesis's premise that Netscape ran a browsable RSS 0.91 channel directory is wrong for this surface, the channel list lived behind the personalised My Netscape page and was never public. **The transferable law qualifies "Aim at 2001, not 1996": head selection is a 2001 problem, not a general one, and the fix is to aim the artifact EARLY.** A control of 20,000 random DISTINCT held `.com` reproduces the register's own 0.611 at 0.5980, validating the measurement, and gives fraction-of-held-missing by year: 1996 0.9231, 1997 0.8423, 1998 0.7933, 1999 0.6048, 2000 0.2605, 2001 0.5980. The same table over Syndic8's 1,527 held publishers: 1999 0.3556, 2000 0.1179, 2001 0.0485. So the head-selection penalty against the population is **12.3x at 2001 and only 1.7x at 1999**, because the store covers long-lived publishers almost completely at 2000 and 2001 and thinly before. The same 1,610 names in an artifact dated 1999 would show 543 held-and-missing instead of 74, a **7.3x swing**. For a curated corpus of survivors, prefer the OLDEST edition; the population rule inverts. The caveat that keeps it honest: missing-1999 partly means "did not exist in 1999", so the headroom is real only for names a genuinely 1999-dated artifact lists, which is why xmlTree was the decisive test and why its unextractability closes the family rather than leaving it open. Two operational finds. **A directory's own machine-readable export clears a whole site in one request**, the `ls-lR` trick applied to a directory: 861,293 B and 3,479 dated rows for one fetch against newsisfree's 1 domain per fetch over the same population, so look for `genfeed`-style OPML or OCS on the front page before crawling any directory tree. And **a Wayback not-archived answer is HTTP 200 with ~4,679 B of calendar HTML**, so status and size cannot separate a hit from a miss and a size-only check silently manufactures a corpus: grep the body for `web-static.archive.org/_static`, which caught 6 false positives on xmlTree that would have read as archived leaf pages. `__wb/sparkline?output=json` retired weblogs.com in a single call with no CDX. Operationally, web.archive.org replay refused connections on ports 80 and 443 repeatedly while `archive.org` answered in 0.65s and a sibling fan-out agent was fetching replay concurrently: **the fan-out competes with itself for one IP's replay budget**, the refusal is connection-level with no `Retry-After`, and a retry-every-25s loop got through where rest-and-double backoff did not. Do not re-test Syndic8, xmlTree, newsisfree, weblogs.com or `my.netscape.com/publish`. |
| **Public Analog and Webalizer reports, the referrer section: the third and last death of traffic as a selection principle (2026-08-28)** | **Zero, and a strong zero: 0 net-new pairs and 0.0 EE before AND after the split, 0 names new even to the candidate pool, from 55 in-window dated reports over 6 live hosts, 7,629,595 B fetched.** The shape is master-eligible and takes no split: the analyser stamps its own period ("Summary Period: March 2001", "Analysed requests from Fri-01-Oct-1999") and a referrer row is a machine observation that the named site sent a real click inside that period, so it is `link_source`. Dating is sound; the value is nil. 61 reports from 9 hosts, 55 dated in window, spanning 1998 16, 1999 17, 2000 16, 2001 6, over US academic, UK and Swedish personal, and an ibiblio-hosted news mirror; largest runs `www.weewave.mer.utexas.edu/stats/logs_{98,99,00}/` 36 monthlies and `space.physics.uiowa.edu/pi/previous/erebus/analog/` 16 quarterlies. Host Reports of client hostnames were excluded by section, being the reverse-DNS visitor-log population already closed at 98.4% and 99.6% held. Referrer sections give **108 distinct registrable domains under a weighted TLD and 141 (domain, year) pairs; 108 of 108 domains already held (100%) and 141 of 141 pairs already held AT the artifact's own year (100%), so held-and-missing-that-year = 0**. `scripts/price_items.py --all-tlds` against merged260827 and an independent duckdb per-domain screen agree exactly at 141/141. Sampled as DISTINCT DOMAINS. TLD mix com 48, edu 20, org 9, jp 5, net 5, au 4, uk 2, gov 2, ca 2, cn 2, so the zero is not an artifact of US academia. **The 2001-threshold law predicts ~84.8 of the 141 pairs should be missing their year (28.1 of the 47 at 2001); observed 0, and P(141/141) under that law is ~1e-56, so this is a different population and not a small sample.** The population, measured: **75.0% of referrer domains are held in ALL six years 1996-2001 against a store baseline of 2.6% on 3,408 random held domains, a 29x enrichment in the longest-lived names**, and reading the list says the same (altavista, aol, google, excite, hotbot, dogpile, goto, alltheweb, geocities, compuserve, earthlink, cnet, adobe, go.com). Three structural facts cap the ceiling independently: (1) **two thirds of reports carry no referrer data at all**, 39 of 58 files never contain the string "referrer" because referrer reporting needs a combined log format, so only 18 of 55 dated reports yield one referrer host; (2) **the report is a truncation that prints what it withheld**, ccic shows 20 of 1,001 referring URLs and says "[not listed: 981 URLs]", chiark 10 of 248, purdue "Top 81 of 125 Total Referrers", so 35% to 98% of the population is suppressed and what is printed is by construction the head; (3) **the class is nearly absent from Wayback**, `www.asi.org/usage/2001-07.html` is a hard 404 in the replay index while `www.asi.org/` has captures, `h-net.org/stats/` redirects to 2012, and `advancedsearch.php` on titles (usage statistics OR webalizer OR web server statistics OR referrer report) AND date:[1996-01-01 TO 2002-12-31] returns numFound 3, being a tucows copy of the Webalizer binary and two ERIC papers. Rule of three on 0 in 141: net-new rate <= 2.13%, so <= 0.0345 EE per dated report and <= 0.1053 EE per referrer-bearing report even if every net-new pair were `.com`; **1,000 EE needs >= 29,004 dated reports (9,492 referrer-bearing) and 5,000 EE needs >= 145,019**, against six searches that named 11 candidate hosts of which 3 no longer resolve and no host serving a directory index. **The transferable law: a referrer is by construction a page that was live, linked, clicked and therefore crawled, so a referrer table is drawn from the crawled population, which is the baseline. Traffic-sending is crawl-correlated, and the correlation is measurable before any download as the fraction of names held in all six years, 75.0% here against a 2.6% baseline.** This kills the referrer field the way 2026-08-25 killed the visitor log and 2026-08-27 killed the counter directory, completing the family from its third and last direction: whichever end of the traffic pipe you read, traffic as a selection principle fails the authority screen. Do not re-test any log-analyser output. Cheaper than sampling: **a log analyser prints the size of the tail it refused to print**, so grep `[not listed:` or `Top N of M` and read the suppression ratio straight off the artifact, and check the Go To list for a Referrer Report at all. New trap: **`archive.org/wayback/available` returns HTTP 200 with `archived_snapshots: {}` for `http://www.yahoo.com/` at timestamp 20010901**, a false zero on a URL Wayback holds thousands of times, so the CDX-free finder recommended on 2026-08-27 needs a control; the replay endpoint's own 302 gets the control right (`web/20010901000000/http://www.yahoo.com/` to `20010901004632`), carries the chosen stamp in `Location`, and needs about 5 retries because web.archive.org drops roughly half of first connections. Search-engine indexes of this class are stale, so treat a hit as a lead and not an artifact (3 of 11 named hosts dead, psubs.org's whole 2001 stats directory 404). robots.txt read in full on every host before any request: ibiblio Crawl-delay 2 with `/ccic/` allowed, uiowa disallows `*/webalizer/` and `*/archive/` but not the `/analog/` path used, `h-net.org` answers 403 on robots.txt and was left alone. |
| **PICS self-rating label registries, closed on the MISSING HALF of the seal-roster discriminator (2026-08-28)** | **Closed at 3.9 net-new post-split EE over 6 pairs, all at 2001, mean weight 0.6451, against a claimed population of order 130,000 self-rated sites.** The family census is complete and it is 11 rating services and 10 label bureaus, taken in TWO requests from `w3.org/PICS/raters.htm` (4,270 B) and `w3.org/PICS/bureaus.htm` (2,939 B) at a 2000-06 capture, which named six services the hypothesis had not: RSACi, SafeSurf, Net Shepherd, SurfWatch, CyberPatrol/Microsystems, EvaluWeb, ORC, Adequate.com, Weburbia, Vancouver Webpages VWP1.0, CSR Bologna, plus IBM, Jigsaw, NewView, Source Pacific and AT&T as bureaus. 47 distinct replay paths fetched, 10 of them 404. **EVERY service is a questionnaire-and-`.rat`-file generator with no directory.** ICRA's entire 2001 site is five nav entries and its only enumeration is `members.html` at 21 corporate hosts (aol, microsoft, bt, novell, uunet, networksolutions); its own About page states "ICRA does not rate internet content, the content providers do that", so the label lives on the labelled site and ICRA holds no roster. `rsac.org` by 2000-06 is a 2,010 B frameset forwarding to ICRA and `ratingsv01.html` is the system definition, not a list. Net Shepherd is a corporate brochure at 1999-04 (4,992 B, 0 external hosts) and 2000-03, by which date it had pivoted to community software. Vancouver Webpages and Weburbia ship a `.rat` file and a header generator, and Weburbia's only site list is a Site Of The Week. `pics.microsys.com` at 1999-06 is a 1,695 B Cyber Patrol shell whose data is the CyberNOT blocklist, the already-closed blocklist lens. So SafeSurf is the only member publishing any site list and those are editorial picks, of the pick shape already closed at line 564. Artifact: 6 pages, 72,309 B, `web/<ts>id_/` at 2001-03-08 to 2001-04-13 over `safesurf.com/kids{1,2,3}.htm`, `safesurf.com/mirror/`, `ufcws.org/menu/index.html` and `child-safe.com/`. Priced by `scripts/price_items.py --all-tlds` against merged260827: 151 distinct pairs over 151 domains, 144 already held, pre-split 7 pairs and 4.9 EE (overstates 1.3x, do not quote), **post-split 6 pairs and 3.9 EE**, com 5 org 1, typo upper bound 85.7%. Sampled as DISTINCT DOMAINS with the six lister domains removed, 147 listed: 146 held any year (99.3%), **141 already carrying 2001 (95.9%)**, so held-and-missing-2001 is 5, 3.24 EE gross, all 5 adjacent (held 2000), agreeing with the split figure to 0.7 EE. **The gap between claimed and enumerable is the whole result: RSACi claimed order-100,000 self-rated sites and SafeSurf order-30,000, and the total enumerable population across the entire family is 147 domains.** **The transferable law: the seal-roster discriminator is necessary but not sufficient, and the missing half is where verification happens.** BBBOnLine and TRUSTe publish the member's own domain because a seal is worth nothing unless a visitor can check it against the issuer, so the issuer MUST run a public directory. A PICS label is verified by the browser parsing the label out of the page itself, so the registry never needs a directory and none of the eleven ever built one. Pre-download question for any "who is certified" corpus: **does the checker go to the ISSUER or to the ARTIFACT? If the artifact carries its own proof, there is no roster, whatever the population size.** Operational, and it changed a number here: **`Memento-Datetime` is what tells you which capture you got, and `%{url_effective}` is not.** `/web/20010401000000id_/http://www.safesurf.com/safesurfing/` returned HTTP 200 with no redirect and served a **2004-03-17** capture while `url_effective` still read `20010401000000id_`; one `curl -I` per page kept a 2004 listing out of a 2001 measurement. Do not re-test ICRA, RSAC, SafeSurf, Net Shepherd, Weburbia, Vancouver Webpages, EvaluWeb, Adequate.com or the Cyber Patrol PICS bureau. Two threads named so nobody chases them: `adequate.com`'s `/cgi-bin/adequate/ratings.cgi` participant list is the only true roster the family ever had and it returns 404 at 1998, 1999, 2000 and 2001 targets, so it exists nowhere; `evaluweb.ptloma.edu` never answered inside the replay budget and is a 1999-era head-selected university service, ceiling well under 10 EE. |
| **2001 trade-show exhibitor rosters, closed: a BOOTH is a head selector (2026-08-28)** | **CLOSED at 7.1 net-new post-split EE over the 214 of 1,103 COMDEX Fall 2001 booth records that were fetched, 0.0438 EE per listed domain. This row supersedes the INCOMPLETE entry filed earlier the same day, whose header figure of 25 had nothing behind it; the pricing has now run.** Dating is unchanged and sound, `dated_directory` shape: the booth record is served from the show's live exhibitor database and replayed at a Wayback timestamp inside 2001, so the artifact asserts the exhibitor's URL as current at an instant the archive fixes, and COMDEX Fall 2001 (12 to 16 November 2001) fixes what the database is a roster OF. Artifact: `http://marketplace.key3media.com/exhibitor/PreviewVirtualBooth.jsp?dept=Attendee&event_id=507&row_id=<opaque Oracle ROWID>`, about 17 kB per record, reached one request deeper than the 27 `ExhibitorList.jsp` pages (1,076,449 B, 1,550 rows) that enumerate the show. **Measurement, sampled as DISTINCT DOMAINS: 214 records, 162 distinct registrable domains, 144 held in some year (88.9%), 130 of those 144 already carry 2001 (90.3% of held), so held-and-missing-2001 is 14, or 9.7% of held, and the post-split total is 7.1 EE.** The premise is what failed, and it failed on the second screen and not the first: the held fraction of 88.9% is authority-corpus grade and fine, but only 9.7% of held names lack 2001 against the population's 61.1% for `.com`, a 6.3x head-selection penalty derived from those two figures. **A paid booth is a head selector.** The hypothesis argued the opposite, that a COMDEX exhibitor list is the B2B long tail and should behave like BBBOnLine's air-conditioning contractors rather than TRUSTe's `about.com`; a company that can fund a stand at the largest trade show in the industry is a company the store already covers at 2001. Cost settles it. At 0.0332 EE per record fetched, the remaining 889 records are worth about 30 EE for about 15 MB, and the whole 1,103-record database about 37 EE, one request per domain, well under the 1 EE per 100 fetches that closed the download catalogues, so the other 889 were not fetched. **The transferable screen is that price of admission predicts the head-selection penalty**: any roster whose entry condition is a payment large enough to notice selects the same population a magazine cites, and the aim-at-2001 rule then pays nothing, exactly as the RSS registries measured on the same day. Do not re-test COMDEX, Internet World, CeBIT, E3, SIGGRAPH or Networld Interop exhibitor directories. |
| **Frozen ccTLD register listings, closed on artifact existence and not on value (2026-08-27)** | **CLOSED at 0 EE, against a 223,193.8 EE ceiling, so the family died on artifact existence and not on weight or value.** All seven named ccTLDs were already answered in this file before the run: `uk` line 593, `au` lines 604 and 779, `nz`, `sg` and `ca` line 604, `ie` line 525 and `us` line 1013 both banked masters. `.ca` was re-tested independently as the largest unbanked ceiling, and closed a second time on the mechanism rather than on a yield: CDNnet exposed the register three ways and none is a listing. (1) `/regs/` and `/search/index.html` are forms POSTing to `/cgi-bin/ca-w-search`, so the register is not enumerable even in principle from a capture. (2) Per-domain static files at `http://www.cdnnet.ca/regs-text/ca.<prov>.<name>`, one URL per domain, and the availability API returns no capture for the directory, its index, or any of three sampled members. (3) `/info/statistics` (19980506062029, 2,326 B) is counts only, zero hostname-shaped tokens, and it hands over a hard volume ceiling: `30,123` registered organizations in the entire `.ca` register at 5 May 1998, against 103,715 distinct in-window `.ca` domains already held, 3.44x, so even a perfect 1998 `.ca` register file is a subset of the store. `.za` third-party academic page closed the same death as the AUNIC page: `www2.frd.ac.za/uninet/zadomains.html` (1999-05-03, 6,066 B), the UNINET manager's own `.ZA` page, is policy prose, 24 distinct `.za` strings of which 17 are second-level labels (`ac.za`, `co.za`, `tm.za`) and the rest worked examples. Ceilings priced against merged260827 sampling DISTINCT DOMAINS via `count(distinct domain)`: held-2000-and-missing-2001 sums to **223,193.8 EE over twelve ccTLDs** (`uk` 117,686.3, `au` 34,991.8, `ca` 29,294.2, `nz` 17,054.0, `za` 12,598.2); bidirectional (held Y-1 or Y+1, missing Y), the correct screen for a register since it attests membership at Y, `uk` 1999 alone is 200,574.8 EE against the 1,623.0 EE that `.jp` dated 1999 actually paid. Of the era's registries only four published bulk at all, IEDR, JPNIC, MYNIC and co.za, and all four are already found. **Method, the one-fetch discriminator that kills a whole namespace: fetch the registry's `reg-services` or how-to-register page, not its front page, and read how it says to retrieve an existing registration. A form POST or a per-domain URL template means the register was never a listing and no capture can make it one; a path with a letter or a scope in it is the `.ie` shape and worth the sweep.** CDNnet handed over both facts in 2,954 bytes, the cheap version of the 170-URL and 1,605-capture enumerations that closed `.nz` and `.au`. Second method piece: `archive.org/wayback/available?url=...&timestamp=YYYYMMDD` separates wrong-path from never-captured WITHOUT touching the metered CDX, because an absent path returns an empty `archived_snapshots` regardless of the timestamp asked for; carry a positive control in the same batch, here `www.isi.edu/in-notes/us-domain-delegated.txt` returning 20010606153725, so the sixteen `none` rows are the host and not the tool (1.2 s spacing, four retries, zero errors in 34 calls). Third: replay returned 0 bytes on the first attempt for two of five paths and the full body on retry, so a zero-byte read is not a missing capture. Do not re-open this on a new ccTLD unless that registry is shown to have published bulk, and test that with the discriminator first; the residual English ccTLDs are under the volume floor at `ph` 4,096 and `in` 2,237 held domains for the whole window, 976.7 and 549.3 EE ceilings even from a perfect 2001 register. Two live threads belong elsewhere: the `.ca` `can.domain` classification ruling at line 745, about 10,482 pairs and 8,768 EE for zero further collection, needing only a human reading of whether a registry-printed `Date-Approved:` field is the registry self-dating; and `.uk`, the largest number here, reachable only through Nominet and closed on their RDAP terms and on the absence of any bulk publication. |
| **Academic and security DNS snapshots that are not ISC, killed by the record layout (2026-08-27)** | **CLOSED at 0 EE, killed by derivation and not by sampling: every non-ISC 1996-2001 DNS and topology instrument records an ADDRESS and not a NAME, so there is no item to date.** (1) Internet Mapping Project (`https://www.cheswick.com/ches/map/` 7,033 B and its format spec `datadoc.html` 5,923 B, robots.txt 99 B allows `/ches/`): the published record format is one line per CIDR block with `Probe=`, `Target=`, `Path=` hop lists, `R`/`S`/`T`/`I` timing and TTL vectors, `Whiner`, `Asnpath`, `Complete`, `Pathdate`, and the only name-bearing label is documented verbatim as "name of the network. Not implemented yet."; the scope paragraph reads "We have no interest in the specific endpoints or network services on those endpoints, just the topology of the 'center' of the Internet." So zero extractable domains from ~90,000 networks probed daily 1998-2011, 0 pairs at any volume, and `dbs/index.html` is 404 with the database "available via bittorent somewhere". (2) Mercator (Govindan and Tangmunarunkit, INFOCOM 2000): hop-limited plus informed random address probing and router-alias resolution, no name field, no release located, and a full release would be router interfaces, the reverse-DNS ISP-infrastructure population already measured at 98.4% and 99.6% held. (3) WAND/WITS (Waikato and Auckland traces from early 1999, the one in-window member): the sanitisation paragraph was obtained before any fetch and is fatal twice, IP addresses anonymised with Crypto-Pan prefix-preserving AES into 10.0.0.0 **and payload removed**, so no DNS question section survives; law 6 kills the addresses and payload stripping kills the names, and the host resolves to 130.217.250.15 but refuses connection on 80 and 443, so nothing was requested. (4) Early passive DNS does not reach the window at all: Weimer's passive DNS replication gathers from 2004 and the earliest commercial product, Sandstorm NetIntercept, is 2002 and proprietary, so no pre-2002 passive DNS corpus exists to test. (5) Archive route, proved against a working control: `advancedsearch.php` on `(title:(dns) OR title:(domain survey) OR title:(hostname) OR title:(host list)) AND date:[1996-01-01 TO 2002-12-31]` returns numFound 92 and 92 docs, classified in full as 41 RFCs, 22 DTIC reports, 7 NASA reports, 3 tucows shareware lookup tools and 19 other (10 O'Reilly/Sybex DNS books, a 1996 Australian statistics capture, a Hungarian audio item, a journal article, a workshop, two hacking texts). **Zero data artifacts**, with the non-zero count as the control that the engine answered. Also reached: MIT/KAIST DNS traces, project page only at `web.archive.org/web/20030429101600id_/http://nms.lcs.mit.edu/dns/` 7,065 B with no data release named, and `nms.lcs.mit.edu` and `nms.csail.mit.edu` no longer resolve. **Method, the pre-fetch discriminator for the whole network-measurement family, one question: does the instrument record a NAME or an ADDRESS? Read the dataset's own format spec or sanitisation paragraph and look for a hostname field; if the record is IP paths, packet headers or anonymised prefixes, the yield is exactly zero regardless of corpus size, era or licence, and no download, no permission request and no sampling is warranted.** It screens out in one pass every 1996-2001 topology and traffic instrument (traceroute maps, skitter, Mercator, Internet Mapping Project, WITS/NLANR PMA, MAWI, DEC-PKT) and it explains why ISC was the only member that ever paid: ISC did zone transfers, and a zone transfer returns names. It is cheaper than law 6, which needs the sanitisation paragraph, because this needs only the record layout, usually one static HTML page on the same host. Second reusable piece: an archive-side zero is a proved zero only if you classify all N returned identifiers rather than counting them, since 92 hits that are 41 RFCs and 10 books read as a live population in a summary and as an empty one in a listing. Do not re-test Internet Mapping Project, Lumeta, Mercator, WAND/WITS, NLANR PMA, MAWI, MIT/KAIST DNS traces or pre-2002 passive DNS on any screen. The one member that could still pay is a pre-February-1999 mirror of RIPE Hostcount host-output files, since RIPE's own README dates the access restriction to 01/02/1999 and a mirror copied before then would hold names; deprioritised rather than untried, because its target years are 1996-1998 and that headroom is 103,953 pairs against 6,708,320 at 2000-to-2001, a 64x penalty. |
| **Seal and certification rosters, the one shape of "customer showcase" that pays (2026-08-27)** | **FIND, 1,581.7 net-new post-split EE over 2,554 pairs, and a discriminator that splits the family in two.** BBBOnLine's Reliability participant directory is an alphabetically enumerable 36-page namespace, `http://www.bbbonline.org/search/Relresult.asp?letter=<@,0-9,A-Z>`, linked from `search/Relbrowse.asp`; 33 letters have a 2001 capture, 3,574,800 bytes, 7,605 participant rows, 9,019 distinct registrable domains under a weighted TLD. Sampled DISTINCT DOMAINS: 8,286 held any year (91.9%), 5,911 already carrying 2001, **2,375 held-and-missing-2001, of which 2,222 (93.6%) are adjacent held-2000-and-missing-2001** rather than death-gap. `scripts/price_items.py --all-tlds` against merged260827: 3,109 net-new pairs and 1,919.7 EE pre-split (do not quote), **2,376 pairs and 1,470.1 EE post-split**, mean weight 0.6187, `com` 2,163 / `net` 175 / `org` 23, typo upper bound 46.3%; an independent duckdb year screen gives 2,375 pairs and 1,469.5 EE, agreeing to 0.04%. TRUSTe's licensee roster (`truste.org/users/users_lookup.html` at `20010603230742`) adds 1,522 domains, 99.0% held, 1,323 already at 2001, **184 held-and-missing-2001 and 115.1 EE**, which is 0.076 EE per listed name against BBBOnLine's 0.163: the head-corpus law exactly, since TRUSTe licensed `abc.com`, `about.com` and `expedia.com` while BBBOnLine listed air-conditioning contractors and local ISPs. **The reusable discriminator: a provider-run member index links the member THROUGH the provider and so cannot name the member's own domain (measured at 0.0 EE the same day, `free_host_member_indexes`), while a seal or certification roster MUST print the member's own domain, because the domain is what is being certified.** So the pre-download question is whether the listing's subject is the customer's SITE or the customer's ACCOUNT, and the whole 7,600-row database comes out in 36 requests with no CDX query anywhere. Two traps paid for here: `archive.org/wayback/available` returns the CLOSEST capture, which for a `20011215` target lands in 2002-01 for a third of the letters and would silently import a 2002 roster as a 2001 observation, so target `20010901` and reject any timestamp not starting `2001`; and this template writes `HREF=http://...` UNQUOTED, so a quoted-href regex reports 0 absolute links on a 347,822-byte page holding 1,013 of them, which is how a real source gets buried. Approval requests filed as `bbbonline_reliability_roster / artifact_listing` at potential 87 and `truste_licensee_roster / artifact_listing` at potential 55, both Decision pending, neither ingested; both readings are on the sheet, since the master reading (a roster generated from a participant database is not something a human typed, the `namewinner_expiring` argument) is 1,919.7 EE against 1,470.1 EE split. Three cheap increments left: the Privacy programme's separate roster at `search/Pribrowse.asp`, the three letters (`L`, `V`, `6`) with no 2001 capture, and the same namespace at 1999 and 2000 captures, where the adjacent-year law says price one letter page before fetching 36. The family is otherwise thin: 12 payment-gateway, storefront and certificate-issuer hosts were screened at 2001 and none carries a merchant listing (`cybercash.com` an 11,247 B shell, `ccbill.com` 1,910 B, `shopnow.com` 983 B, `ibill.com` only a `/Members/` login, `authorizenet.com` no directory link, `miva.com` and `mercantec.com` link a storefront), and `dotcomdirectory.com` delegates every category listing to `dotcomdirectory.net`, which has NO capture at all. |
| **IRC logs, web forums and signature URLs, closed on density at a scale that cannot be exceeded (2026-08-27)** | **511.1 net-new post-split EE over 800 pairs, and the family is closed because conversation is the wrong density and scale does not fix it.** Four artifacts, all fetched and all priced against merged260827: the Archive Team grab of Salon Table Talk (`archiveteam-tabletalk-panic/tabletalk.final.tar.bz2`, 923,956,129 B, 156,894 HTML pages), an Offline Explorer mirror of `forums.somethingawful.com` taken 2001-04-30 to 2001-05-01 (`something-awful-forums-2001/SA2001.zip`, 103,117,451 B), 303,879 FidoNet echomail messages (`fidonet_combined_execpc_starfleet_messages_1995_1999`, 530,898,445 B raw), and `nirvs-clone-cd-irc-logs` (#clonecd on EFnet, 2000-12-29 to 2003-07-03). Family union 20,761 distinct domains, 26,360 of 28,348 pairs held (93.0%), mean weight 0.6388, 530 of the 800 net-new pairs at 2001; gross is 1,988 pairs and 1,300.6 EE, do not quote. **Table Talk is the decisive test because nothing else in this family reaches its scale: 7,195,164 posts carry a WebX machine stamp, 2,330,700 of them in window (32.4%) and 885,916 at 2001, and it paid 224.5 EE over 355 pairs, so 0.0000963 EE per in-window post and 1,000 EE needs 10.4 million dated posts.** Of the 10,501 domains it names that the store holds at some in-window year, only 713 (6.8%) lack 2001, against the 61.1% `.com` population average, and those 713 spread over 697 distinct posts with at most four from any single post: evenly distributed, millions of items, and it still prices here. Something Awful looks 20x better and the reason is not the forum: 167 held-and-missing-2001 pairs over its page text, but **121 of them (72.5%) come from ONE page**, `showthread.php@threadid=26538`, a 616,004 B pasted adult-paysite login list, and the other 7,713 pages of genuine 2001 conversation give 46 pairs and 28.1 EE: **0.0036 EE per page against 7.6 EE per page for the ten dump pages, a 2,100x gap.** FidoNet is the same lens in the wrong years (85,980 / 113,037 / 79,469 messages at 1996 / 1997 / 1998, and 2 after), paying 127.3 EE on message bodies and 7.6 EE over 277,168 tearlines, because a 1996-1998 `* Origin:` line carries a node number and a phone number and almost never a hostname; its 0.0418 pairs per item lands exactly on the 0.042 prose ceiling while net-new post-split is 61x below it, so that ceiling describes extraction and not yield. CloneCD refutes the hypothesis's own premise, since the machine channel outpaid the typed one: hostmask `***` lines (reverse DNS) 25 pairs and 15.4 EE against typed `<nick>` lines 20 pairs and 12.1 EE. Per listed domain the family lands on the head-selection constant and then falls below it: SA conversation 0.0480, CloneCD 0.0391, **Table Talk 0.0203**, FidoNet 0.0155, against Bomis rings 0.0481, WinNetMagCD 0.041 and 0.386 for a random held `.com` missing 2001. So 1,000 EE needs about 21,000 distinct domains in one 2001-dated artifact and the two 2001 artifacts here hold 2,341 and 11,081. **Negatives, each against a passing control.** The Archive Team Yahoo! Groups API grab is the one lead in the family with the volume to matter (hundreds of items at 30 to 45 GB, per-message dates back to eGroups in 1998) and it is DARK rather than missing: three megawarcs return 401, 401 and 403 on HEAD, the item CDX derivative returns 401, and a Range request 302s into the same wall, while the control `googlegroups-part2-S` in a sibling Archive Team collection lists a downloadable 45,043,937,280 B tar. `slashdot.org` robots.txt disallows `/comments.pl`, `/stories/`, `/journal` and `/search`; `kuro5hin.org` returns an empty body; `advogato.org` 302s its own robots.txt to `web.archive.org`. **No other in-window IRC log corpus exists** (`title:(irc log)` 37 items, `identifier:irclog*` 23, every one 2003 or later bar CloneCD and a 1995 item; `logbot.info` begins 2004 by its own description) and **no other in-window forum mirror exists** (`("Offline Explorer" OR "HTTrack" OR "wget mirror") AND date:[1996 TO 2002]` is 26 items of which exactly one is a forum, the rest magazine cover CDs; `mediatype:web AND date:[1996 TO 2002]` is 4,476 items that die on law 1, and excluding `title:(Crawldata)` leaves 256 Archive-It crawls run in 2021 and 2022). FidoNet NODELISTS were closed on date at line 784 of this file; this measures the MESSAGE BASE and confirms the same sub-500 EE family ceiling from the other side. Method, five reusable pieces: (1) **split one corpus by WHO WROTE THE HOSTNAME before pricing, not after**, which is what refuted the premise here; (2) **attribute yield to items before quoting a per-item rate**, one `Counter(first_item[d] for d in net_new)` turning a 104 EE forum into 28.1 EE of forum plus 76 EE of one pasted list, and running the same screen on Table Talk proved the opposite there, which is how two anecdotes became one law; (3) an Offline Explorer or HTTrack zip preserves the downloader's per-file mtimes, so a saved response body stamped 2001 is a CAPTURE rather than a `link_target` and needs no corroboration from the page, and `unzip -l` alone prices a 103 MB artifact without extracting it; (4) **a prefix of a hash-ordered tar is not a sample**, since the first 80,000 of Table Talk's 156,894 pages read 0.6% in-window against 32.4% for the whole corpus because the old forums cluster under their own hash prefixes, so stream the whole `r|bz2` member by member and drop every post with no hostname, turning 7.2 GB into a 166,047-item file in one pass; (5) HEAD the largest file of an `archiveteam` item before budgeting its download, with a sibling collection as the 200 control, because privacy-sensitive grabs go dark while their metadata stays public and searchable. Closed: do not re-test IRC logs, web forums, BBS echomail, signature URLs or guestbooks on any screen. The 511.1 EE is real and sits in four named, licence-clear, correctly dated artifacts if the banking process wants it, Table Talk and Something Awful carrying 355 and 210 of the 800 pairs. |
| Award galleries and pick-of-the-day archives, re-aimed at 2001 and closed a third time (2026-08-27) | **Closed at 3.5 net-new post-split EE over 5 pairs, reproducing the 2026-08-08 reading to within 0.34 EE on a disjoint set of pages.** The class was already closed twice in this file ("Award galleries and cool-site lists", 206 domains over 7 pages, 3.16 EE; and "Dated directories and navigation sites", 2026-08-24), and it was re-tested anyway because both closures predate the 2001-threshold law and every artifact behind them was dated in a year already well covered. The loophole does not open. 8 dated editions over 6 programs, 197,041 B, fetched raw as `web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/`: `coolsiteoftheday.com` 2001-05-22, `goldenwebawards.com` 2001-06-01, `usatoday.com/life/cyber/ch.htm` 2001-05-30, `projectcool.com/sightings/` 2001-05-19, `links2go.com` 2001-05-24 and `yahoo.com/new/` plus `/picks/` at 1997 and 1998. Dating is fine and is a `dated_directory` of the `ncsa_whats_new` shape, the edition date being printed in body (`05/30/2001 - Updated 02:56 PM ET`) or carried in the path (`/life/cyber/2001-05-30-free-e-mail.htm`). The population is not: priced by `scripts/price_items.py --all-tlds`, 135 distinct pairs over 128 distinct domains, 111 already held (86.7%), net-new pre-split 24 pairs and 13.2 EE (do not quote, overstates 3.8x), **post-split 5 pairs and 3.5 EE**, all 5 at 2001, mean weight 0.7011, typo upper bound 37.5%. **That is 0.44 EE per dated edition, and the mechanism is that the family's two halves fail on OPPOSITE screens.** Sampled as distinct domains: the head half (portal and newspaper editorial picks, 43 distinct) is 42 held at 97.7% and **42 of 42 held names already carry 2001, so held-AND-missing-2001 is 0**; the tail half (award-program winners off the 2001-06-01 Golden Web Awards page, 28 distinct) is 71.4% held with 28.6% novel names that take the corroboration split and earn no year, leaving 4 held-and-missing-2001. What the head lists is already dated at 2001, what the tail lists is novel and undatable. **Volume ceiling, measured rather than assumed, from `__wb/sparkline?output=json` in ONE request per host and no CDX at all**: in-window captures are 345 for `usatoday.com/life/cyber/ch.htm` (303 of them 2001), 58 for `projectcool.com/sightings/` and 48 for `goldenwebawards.com`, so a page rewritten daily for six years survives as 58 distinct editions, about one per 38 days. Granting the whole family 8 programs at 150 in-window editions each is 1,200 pages and about 530 EE against a 5,000 EE bar, before marginal novelty collapses on the affiliate plumbing every edition repeats (`bfast.com`, `linksynergy.com`, `commission-junction.com`, `flycast.com`; `links2go.com` yielded exactly 1 registrable domain and it was an ad network). Reaching 5,000 EE would need about 11,400 dated editions. **The reusable discriminator is entries per edition and who chose them, not how many years the program ran**: `ncsa_whats_new` is banked at 4,916 names for 1996 because it is an ANNOUNCEMENT REGISTER enumerating a population at about 400 entries per issue, while an editorial PICK archive is 1 to 33 registrable domains per edition with a third of those its own ad plumbing, and on the best member the picked URL is not on the pick page at all but one level deeper in a dated review article, so the real cost is about 1 request per 1 to 3 domains, the same structure that made `point.lycos.com` measure 1 outbound domain per 484-href page. **Two false zeros caught, both by positive control**: `archive.org/wayback/available` began answering `{"archived_snapshots": {}}` for a URL whose 2001 capture had already been fetched and parsed, which also invalidates the two zeros recorded for `/life/cyber/arch.htm` in that same window; and `yahoo.com/new/` first read as 0 external domains under an `href="http..."` regex because Yahoo wrapped every outbound link as `href="/new/*http://..."`, where a permissive scan for `https?://` anywhere in the document gave 19 domains. Closed: do not re-test picks, galleries or award programs. If anything in this direction is worth an hour it is the REGISTER variant rather than the pick variant, a 1999-2001 successor to NCSA What's New listing all newly launched sites rather than the chosen few, which is a different population. |
| Download-site catalogues with per-entry dates, closed on fetch cost rather than yield (2026-08-27) | **Closed at 1.264 net-new post-split EE over 13 leaf pages, 0.0972 EE per fetch and 1.00 vendor domain per fetch.** Half the family was already closed at line 837 of this file (Winsite `INDEX.TXT`, 7,057 entries and zero vendor domains; CNET Download.com, zero vendor URLs; SimTel, no author domains). Of the rest, WinFiles is dead as an independent site by 2001, its 2001-04-05 capture being a 1,810-byte CNET shell that links only download.com, so it collapses into the closed Download.com entry; and Jumbo, FileMine, SoftSeek, DaveCentral, freewarehome and completelyfreesoftware were all captured in 2001 and all fail the density screen. **No site in this family prints the vendor homepage on a multi-entry page**: a SoftSeek category page is 24,499 B with 8 distinct external hosts, all zdnet and gamespot furniture; freewarehome's front page is a 716-byte frameset; completelyfreesoftware's `index_all.html` is 47,112 B with 14 external hosts, 12 of them ads and webrings; a 32bit.com category page is 45,143 B with 7 external hosts, all its own. Only the per-program LEAF prints the vendor domain, and it prints exactly one. 32bit.com is the one surviving member, dated the Bomis way with `Date Added: MM/DD/YY` beside `Home Page: <vendor url>` and the Wayback capture pinning the page, a `dated_directory`; its tree is 9 category pages, 190 subcategories, 19 leaf IDs per subcategory, IDs running 491 to 27,449. 13 leaf pages priced against merged260827, sampling distinct domains: 13 of 13 carry both fields, 13 of 13 domains already held (100%), 12 of 13 already held AT 2001 (92.3%), held-and-missing-the-catalogue's-own-year 2 (`mvsoft-comp.com` 2000, `fileportal.com` 2001), **net-new post-split 1.264 EE**. The `Date Added` years scatter 1999 x5, 2000 x4, 2001 x4, so only a third of the corpus can even offer the one year with headroom. n=13 is small and quoted as such, but it lands with two independent head-selected measurements (Bomis rings 0.0481 EE per name, WinNetMagCD 0.041) against 0.386 for a random held `.com`: a software catalogue lists the vendor still worth listing, and the store covers those at 2001. At 0.0972 EE and one domain per fetch, 1,000 EE needs about 10,300 leaf fetches, 20x the fetch cost of a list artifact of the same value. Method: **read the rendered text of the LEAF, but screen on the LIST**, one request per site counting distinct external hosts on a category page, which disqualified six sites in seven requests. Two operational findings. **web.archive.org replay refuses concurrency**: six parallel urllib workers returned 9 non-429 errors and zero content, six parallel curl workers got `Failed to connect ... port 80` on 25 of 37, while a single serial curl to the same path returned 200 and 33,697 bytes in the same minute. It is a connection refusal, not a rate limit that answers `Retry-After`, so budget replay as ONE serial client at about 4s per page (~900/hour), not as a pool; serial urllib is a further 6x slower than serial curl on these redirecting `id_` paths, so use `curl -L`. And `/web/<ts>id_/` on this host 302s to the nearest capture, so a 404 means the path was never archived while a 302 means it was, separating "not captured" from "not on the site" without touching CDX. Closed: do not re-test Download.com, SimTel, Winsite, WinFiles, Jumbo, FileMine, SoftSeek, DaveCentral, freewarehome or completelyfreesoftware. The only unfinished thread is 32bit.com's `/software/listings/Authors/`, which would be the dense artifact if it exists but 404s at both 2001-06-01 and 2001-07-20 and appears only inside an HTML comment on the pages naming it, so it was probably never live; one CDX filename query settles it. If anyone does spend the fetches on the leaf tree, rank the queue by `Date Added` year, since the 1999 and 2000 two-thirds is worth roughly nothing. |
| **Bruce Guenter's spam archive re-priced: the advertised URL is refuted and the RECIPIENT header is the find (2026-08-27)** | **1,288.1 net-new post-split EE over 3,053 pairs, 6.6x the 2026-08-15 reading of the same bytes, and the hypothesis that produced it is refuted with the sign reversed.** The lens was the URL a spam ADVERTISES rather than the sender it forges, on the argument that a spammer paying for traffic must name a real live site. Five populations were cut out of the same 20,010 messages (`untroubled.org/spam/1998.7z` through `2001.7z`, 9,312,329 B, plus two 1997-1998 header dumps) and priced separately by `scripts/price_items.py --all-tlds`, sampling DISTINCT DOMAINS: recipient (To/Cc/Bcc/Delivered-To) 10,313 domains, **91.1% held**, mean weight 0.549, 566.2 EE; sender (asserted plus all relays) 15,379, 82.7%, 680.9 EE; observed (last-hop `Received:` only) 8,999, 82.6%, 485.5 EE; asserted (From/Return-Path/Message-ID) 7,486, 83.3%, 219.7 EE; **body (advertised URLs) 4,820 domains, 77.3% held, the WORST of the five, 139.6 EE**; union 26,112 domains, 29,356 pairs, 83.9% held, **1,288.1 EE**. The advertised domain is disposable, registered days before the campaign, so it is exactly the name the store does not hold, and under the split not holding it earns nothing: 678 of its 4,820 names are new even to the candidate pool. **Two laws come out of this.** (1) **Forgery does not predict the held fraction at all.** What the receiver observed on the wire (peer hostname, HELO, IP, not under the spammer's control) reads 82.6% and what the sender asserted reads 83.3%, indistinguishable. So the register's 4.56% figure from the Lazarus remailer logs is not a law about forged headers, it is a property of remailer nym addresses; direct-to-MX bulk mail forges plausible real ISP domains because it wants to look deliverable, and its forged senders are 18x more likely to be held. **Ask which FIELD, not whether it is forged**: established business or ISP (recipient, pays), Asian open relay (observed, weight 0.313), disposable landing site (body, 77.3%). (2) **The harvested recipient list is the highest-value field in a message**, 91.1% held at weight 0.549, the only population high on both screens, because a harvested address sits at an established English-language business that survived, and 2001 spam BCCs an alphabetical walk of somebody's zone rather than human-typed names. Dating: the qmail maildir filename is a unix epoch written by the RECEIVING MTA and the last-hop `Received:` line restates it; 20,007 of 20,010 filename epochs agree with the archive's own directory year, and the spammer's forgeable `Date:` header is ignored. 2001 carries 2,794 of the 3,053 pairs (1998 115, 2000 91, 1999 50, 1997 3) because 14,732 of the 20,010 messages are 2001; by TLD com 1,353, jp 245, net 194, kr 193, tw 131, de 102. Adjacent-year check: 1,106.8 EE, 85.9%, sits on a domain held at Y-1 or Y+1, so it is not the contaminated held-any-year shape. Gross before the split is 7,325 pairs and 3,470.5 EE: do not quote. Ten random scoring pairs were traced to the byte that dates them and all ten confirmed. **Why the 2026-08-15 row read 195.5 EE over 4,793 domains and these bytes hold 26,112: nothing was MIME-decoded.** 2001 spam hides its URL in base64 or quoted-printable HTML, and an `email` walk with `get_payload(decode=True)` over every text/* part yields 78,753,891 bytes of body and a URL in 11,734 of 20,010 messages where a raw grep finds almost none. **A filename epoch is a delivery stamp only in a maildir**: all 401 files in `1997-1998-headers.tar.bz2` carry epochs inside 89 seconds of 1998-03-26, which is when the mailbox was dumped, and only the mbox `From ` separator in the sibling reaches 1997. Licence: "Permission is hereby granted to use this archive without restriction", robots.txt 50 B denying only `/stats/` and `/lists/`. **The family is exhausted**: the `untroubled_spam_archive` item on archive.org is byte-identical file for file, `spamarchive.org` is now a Syracuse window-replacement business and so is a proved zero rather than a refusal, and Ling-Spam is 481 messages and body-only. Sizing for a future trap: 0.0644 EE and 1.30 distinct domains per message, so 5,000 EE needs about 78,000 in-window trapped messages. Raised for approval as `untroubled_spam_headers / artifact_listing`. **Re-run the MIME and recipient laws over every mail-shaped corpus already ingested** (`data/raw/enron`, `data/raw/maillists`, `data/raw/usenet`): anything ingested from HTML hypermail never saw a header at all. |
| **Bomis rings, and the redirector law that does not generalise (2026-08-27)** | **19.1 net-new post-split EE over 16 archived ring pages, and the finding is that a redirector kill is a claim about one hub rather than about a shape.** `docs/sources.md` rejected webring member lists on 2026-08-05 because every WebRing member is linked through `go.webring.org`. Bomis routes its members through its own `/rings/<ring>/<n>` pages too, but it ALSO prints the bare hostname as text beside each title (`3. The Rotherwick Firewall Resource   www.zeuros.co.uk`), so the domain is extractable with no second request and the redirector is irrelevant. 20 rings drawn with a fixed seed from the 83 named on the 14 top-level `/tree/` pages, fetched as `http://web.archive.org/web/20010701000000id_/http://www.bomis.com/ring_home.fcgi?ring=<ring>`, 6-8 KB each: 16 returned content, all captured 2001-04 to 2001-07, so **in-window archival coverage is 16/20 = 80%**. 500 host strings gave **396 distinct registrable domains, 392 held at some year (98.99%), 361 already held AT 2001, held-and-missing-2001 = 31, only 4 names never held, 19.1 EE post-split** (re-priced against the live store on harvest: 405 domains, 35 raw pairs, 31 after the split, mean weight 0.615, all at 2001, com 28 and net 3). Rates: **0.0481 EE per listed domain, 0.95 EE per ring attempted, 1.19 per productive ring, 24.8 domains per ring**, and five of the first six rings paid exactly zero while one shopping ring carried 15.27 of the 19.06. **It is thin for the head-selection reason and the figure reproduces one the register already has**: only 7.9% of held names lack 2001 here (31/392) against the population average of 61.1% for `.com`, which is the `WinNetMagCD` result from another direction (0.0481 EE per name against that magazine's 0.041, against 0.386 for a random held `.com`). A curated ring lists the site still worth listing, and the store covers those at 2001. Negatives measured on the way, each against a passing control: **RingSurf dies on retrieval, not on shape**, its 2001 front page claiming 4,300 rings and 22,000 sites and its directory naming rings as `/netring?ring=X;action=info`, yet **0 of 16 sampled ring pages are archived at all**, with the control being the WebRing URL the register already records (`www.webring.com/cgi-bin/webring?ring=railring&list`), returned at its exact recorded capture `20000422003921` by the same API; **LinkExchange** has no 2001 surface, closest capture 1999-08-30. Method, three reusable pieces: (1) before writing off any member list, **read the rendered TEXT and not only the `href`s**, because hubs printed the hostname for the reader even where the anchor pointed at their own counter, and that one check reopened a family closed for three weeks; (2) **`archive.org/wayback/available` substitutes for CDX** when CDX is metered by other collectors, since it accepts a full query string and honours a `timestamp=` pin, but validate it against a capture the register already records before trusting a zero; (3) **pin the replay timestamp explicitly**, because `/web/2001id_/` resolved to 2002-01-22 for a page that has a 2001-06-01 capture and would have silently bought an out-of-window corpus, while `/web/20010701000000id_/` returned 20 of 20 captures inside 2001-04 to 2001-07. Volume is the one thing unmeasured: at 0.95 EE per ring, 1,000 EE needs ~1,050 rings and 5,000 EE needs ~5,250, and a BFS of `/tree/` had 164 unvisited category pages queued after 4 fetches, with several rings auto-imported ODP mirrors, so the inventory is plausibly in the thousands. `www.bomis.com/rings/<r>/` carries the member count as `max=N` in its frame src, so ring SIZE is readable one request before the member list and the queue can be ranked biggest-first, which matters because the yield is concentrated in the few large rings. The blocker is IA replay throughput, not permission. Raised for approval as `bomis_ring_member_lists / dated_directory`. Do not spend more time on RingSurf, LinkExchange or WebRing itself. |
| **Free-hosting and ISP member indexes, re-tested at 2001 on the right screen (2026-08-27)** | **Still 0.0 EE, and now for the right reason, plus a mechanism that generalises.** The 2026-08-08 closure used the RETIRED novelty screen ("617 domains at 97.4% already held"), written 17 days before the 2001-threshold law, which is the same error corrected for Stanford WebBase, so it was worth one re-test. Four fresh artifacts plus three host homepages as controls, all reachable: Tripod's member index at `20010515062824` (173 B), GeoCities' neighbourhoods at `20000901091820` (567 B), Bigstep's example sites at `20010603211725` (17,627 B), NetNation's `/customer/` at `20010601115834` (15,861 B), and `pair.com`, `he.net`, `netnation.com` at 2001. `scripts/price_items.py` over all seven against the live store: **7 items, all in window, 15 distinct registrable domains, 16 pairs, 16 of 16 already held, 0 net-new before AND after the split, 0.0 EE**. Year screen on distinct domains: 15/15 held, **15 already carrying 2001, held-and-missing-2001 = 0**. Every one of the 15 is a provider or ad-network name (`tripod.com`, `geocities.com`, `bigstep.com`, `netnation.com`, `pair.com`, `he.net`, `bfast.com`, `yimg.com`), 12 of them held across all six years. **The mechanism is the transferable part: a provider-run member index links members through the PROVIDER, so it cannot name a member's own domain even when the member has one.** Tripod's index is a 301 stub naming `tripod.com`; GeoCities' is a meta-refresh into `cgi-bin/hood/geo`, so there is no bulk index artifact at all. **The vanity-domain carve-out was tested on its best instance and refuted**: Bigstep hosted small BUSINESSES with their own domains and showcases 13 members in 17,627 bytes while naming **zero** of their domains, because each member is a thumbnail (`ss_bookbouquet.gif`) linked through bigstep.com; exactly one member name was recoverable anywhere on the page, from a caption, and the store holds it at 2000 and 2001. NetNation's `/customer/` is a login portal with four hostnames, all NetNation's own, and `pair.com` and `he.net` carry no customer-list navigation at 2001. This is the same death WebRing took, reached on a host whose members were businesses rather than hobbyists, which is the strongest form of the carve-out. Volume kills what the mechanism leaves: at 1 recoverable domain per 13 showcased members, the ~2,590 held `com` names 1,000 EE needs at 2001 would take about 33,600 members over roughly 2,600 pages. Ten guessed customer-list paths returned NONE from the availability API, but a positive control proved that was wrong paths and not missing captures: the same API returns 2001 captures for all three bare hosts. Method worth keeping: **`archive.org/wayback/available?url=...&timestamp=YYYYMMDD` finds captures without touching a metered CDX** (`web.archive.org` returns 404 for robots.txt so carries no file; `archive.org`'s is 12 lines, `/control/` and `/report/` only, naming no Claude agent), and **fetch the `id_` replay form** or Wayback's link rewriting turns every outbound href into `web.archive.org` and the extraction measures the archive instead of the artifact. A retry wrapper is required: `web.archive.org` refused the connection on 6 of 14 attempts and succeeded on retry every time. The one population left in this family is NameZero, which registered real `.com` domains for over 400,000 free members by May 2000 and was then the largest single registrant in the world; its 2001 site publishes no member directory, so it is unreachable through this lens and would have to come from registry or WHOIS data, where the Verisign pool is already spent. Do not re-test free-hosting or ISP member indexes on any screen. |
| **Site Meter and the counter-service lens, re-proposed and closed without a fetch (2026-08-27)** | **0 EE, duplicate lens.** The hypothesis named theCounter, NedStat, eXTReMe Tracking and Site Meter; the counter-directory row above sizes the first three by name in the same sweep (`nedstat.com` 3,392 in-window captures, `extreme-dm.com` 7, `thecounter.com` 0) and closes the family on measurement: `hitbox.com`'s head page at 1998-02-05 gives 48 registrable domains, **48 of 48 already held AT 1998, 0.0 EE**, the deep rank pages carry 0 member rows, and the whole in-window list-page universe is 483 captures with **none at 2001**. Site Meter is the only member never named in `docs/sources.md` (grepped `sitemeter|site meter|statcounter|webtrends|hitbox|websidestory` across `docs/`, one hit). It is not an exception: sitemeter.com launched 1999 and its public surface is per-member stats pages plus a ranked top-sites list, the identical shape, and the closure's mechanism is structural rather than host-specific. No new measurement was taken and none is warranted. **The cheap pre-download test this leaves behind: a service's public list is not its database, it is the marketing head of it.** The only version of this family that could ever pass the authority screen is the inverse shape, a public artifact that is NOT rank-ordered so that publication is not head-selection: a full alphabetical member index, an embedder manifest, or a billing list. Do not re-test traffic or counter services. |
| **The "Wayback refused this IP" hour, measured: it was not us, not a block, and not that hour (2026-08-27)** | **The refusal is real but it is REPLAY ONLY, and the concurrency hypothesis is refuted three ways.** The flag said `web.archive.org` refused this IP at the TCP layer through most of the 15:00 UTC hour while `ark cdx` ran two clients. Four corrections, all measured. **(1) The hour is wrong by two.** Every log this project writes is local CEST, so the window was **13:05 to 13:35 UTC**, and anyone correlating it against the archive's own status history would have read the wrong hour. **(2) There was one `ark cdx` client, not two**: `cdx_pool` stopped at 08:02Z and only `cdx_gtail` was running; the second client was the iteration's OWN replay fetcher, sequential, one socket, started 13:10:28Z. **(3) Throughput is invariant to our concurrency.** Six batches today, worker count 8, 8, 3, 3, 3, 8: rates 8.93, 10.03, 8.90, 9.05, 7.16 and 8.31 domains/min. Cutting workers from 8 to 3 at 10:46Z changed the rate by 0.3% and the failure rate went the WRONG way, 3.67% to 6.17% to 7.83% to 18.45%, then FELL to 8.62% when workers went back to 8. A 2.7x change in our own in-flight requests moves neither number, because `throttles` ran at roughly one per query all day and `final_delay_ms` was pinned at its 3.0s ceiling in every completed batch: the bottleneck is server-side per-query latency shared across workers, so we were never the limiting party. **(4) It was not an IP-level block, because the index kept answering.** In the incident batch the CDX index still completed 233 queries and answered **81.5% of them 200**. A block on this IP would take both services; instead, in the same minutes, `web.archive.org/cdx/search/cdx` ran at ~8.3 domains/min while `web.archive.org/web/<ts>id_/` produced **21 successes in 26.9 minutes, then zero in the 5m10s to 13:42:32Z**. The two are different tiers and only the replay tier fell over. **It also did not end at 13:35Z**: the replay client was still alive and still producing nothing seven minutes later, so "most of that hour" understates the duration and misattributes the boundary to the supervisor restart that happened to fall there. **The reusable finding is the forensic gap: a per-minute series cannot be reconstructed from the cdx journals at all.** Journal records carry `domain`, `status`, `strategy`, `truncated` and `years` and **no timestamp**; the only per-second stream is the tqdm progress bar on stderr, which `supervise_cdx_pool.sh` strips with `grep -v "domain/s]"` before appending to the shared log, and `BATCH_OUT` is truncated on every dispatch. So the finest retrospective resolution this project can ever reach on a past window is one batch, about an hour. The supervisor already computes `journal_bytes()` every `CHECK` and logs it only on a stall; logging it unconditionally would give a 15-minute series for free. **Do not make that edit while a supervisor is running**: bash reads a script lazily by byte offset, so editing a live `supervise_cdx_pool.sh` can corrupt the loop mid-flight. **The operational rule: when the archive slows, do not throttle ourselves further. Check the index separately from replay first, because only one of them is usually down, and our worker count is not what either responds to.** |
